\mathcal{A}_{NT}=\left\{\left|\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{e}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\right|_{\infty}\leq\omega_{1}/2\right\}
and
\mathcal{B}_{NT}=\left\{|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{x}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}|_{\mathrm{max}}\leq\frac{C_{0}}{16}\sqrt{\log d/(NT)}\right\}
for some constant
C_{0}>0
. Note that by using Lemma
1
(1) and
\omega_{1}\asymp\sqrt{\log d/(NT)}
, the event
\mathcal{A}_{NT}=\left\{|\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{e}^{\top}\mathbf{X}|_{\infty}\leq\omega_{1}/2\right\}
holds with probability larger than
C_{NT}
for some
C_{1},C_{2}>0
. Similarly, by using Lemma
A.4
(1) and
x=\frac{C_{0}}{16}\sqrt{\log d/(NT)}
for some constant
C_{0}>0
,
\mathcal{B}_{NT}=\left\{|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{x}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}|_{\mathrm{max}}\leq x\right\}
holds with probability larger than
C_{NT}
for some
C_{1},C_{2}>0
. Hence, we have

\Pr(\mathcal{A}_{NT}\cap\mathcal{B}_{NT})=\Pr(\mathcal{A}_{NT})+\Pr(\mathcal{B}_{NT})-\Pr(\mathcal{A}_{NT} \cup\mathcal{B}_{NT})\geq C_{NT}.

By the nature of minimizing procedure in (
2.2
), we have

\frac{1}{2NT}|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|_{2}^{2}+\omega_{1}|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|_{1}\leq\frac{1}{2NT}|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{2}^{2}+\omega_{1}|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{1}.

Since
\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{e}^{\top}\mathbf{X}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0})\leq|\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{e}^{\top}\mathbf{X}|_{\infty}|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{1}\leq\frac{\omega_{1}}{2}|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{1}
, we have

\frac{1}{NT}|\mathbf{X}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0})|_{2}^{2}+2\omega_{1}|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|_{1}\leq\omega_{1}|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{1}+2\omega_{1}|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{1}.

Note that
|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{1}+|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{1}-|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|_{1}=|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{J}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,J}|_{1}+|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,J}|_{1}-|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{J}|_{1}\leq 2|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{J}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,J}|_{1}
, we have

\frac{1}{NT}|\mathbf{X}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0})|_{2}^{2}+\omega_{1}|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{1}\leq 4\omega_{1}|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{J}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,J}|_{1},

and thus

\frac{1}{NT}|\mathbf{X}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0})|_{2}^{2}+\omega_{1}|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{J^{c}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,J^{c}}|_{1}\leq 3\omega_{1}|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{J}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,J}|_{1}.

By using Lemma
A.5
(1) and the condition
s\leq\frac{\psi_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}}(J)}{2C_{0}}\sqrt{\frac{NT}{\log d}}
, we have

\frac{1}{NT}|\mathbf{X}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0})|_{2}^{2}\geq(\psi_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}}(J)-C_{0}s\sqrt{\log d/(NT)})|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{2}^{2}\geq\frac{\psi_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}}(J)}{2}|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{2}^{2}

conditional on the event
\mathcal{A}_{NT}\cap\mathcal{B}_{NT}
.

Hence,

\frac{\psi_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}}(J)}{2}|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{2}^{2}\leq 4\omega_{1}|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{J}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,J}|_{1}\leq 4\omega_{1}\sqrt{s}|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{J}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,J}|_{2}\leq 4\omega_{1}\sqrt{s}|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{2},

which follows that
|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{2}\leq\frac{8\sqrt{s}\omega_{1}}{\psi_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}}(J)}

and

|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{1}\leq 4|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{J}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,J}|_{1}\leq 4\sqrt{s}|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{J}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,J}|_{2}\leq\frac{32s\omega_{1}}{\psi_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}}(J)}.

(2). Similar to part (1), this proof is conditional on the event
\mathcal{A}_{NT}\cap\mathcal{B}_{NT}
.

Define
\vec{\mathbf{b}}=(\sgn(\beta_{j})g_{j})_{j\in J}
. Due to the property of convex optimization,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a solution if and only if there exists a subgradient

\vec{\mathbf{g}}\in\partial\sum_{j=1}^{d}g_{j}|\widehat{\beta}_{j}|=\left\{\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\mid z_{j}=\sgn(\widehat{\beta}_{j})g_{j}\ \text{for}\ \widehat{\beta}_{j}\neq 0\ \text{and}\ |z_{j}|\leq g_{j}\ \text{elsewhere}\right\}

such that
\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{w}-\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}+\omega_{1}\vec{\mathbf{g}}=\mathbf{0}
.

Hence,
\sgn(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{w})=\sgn(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0})
if and only if

\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{J^{c}}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J}\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{w,J}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,J}\right)-\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{J^{c}}^{\top}\mathbf{e}=-\omega_{1}\vec{\mathbf{g}}_{J^{c}},

\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J}\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{w,J}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,J}\right)-\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{e}=-\omega_{1}\vec{\mathbf{g}}_{J}=-\omega_{1}\vec{\mathbf{b}},

\sgn(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{w,J})=\sgn(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,J})\hskip 9.24994pt\text{and}\hskip 9.24994pt\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{w,J^{c}}=\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,J^{c}}=\mathbf{0},

where
\mathbf{X}_{J}
denotes the sub-matrix of
\mathbf{X}
only containing the columns indexed by
J
, and
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,J}
denotes the sub-vector of
\boldsymbol{\beta}
containing the elements indexed by
J
.

By standard results in matrix perturbation theory, Lemma
A.5
(1) and
s\leq\frac{\psi_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}}(J)}{2C_{0}}\sqrt{\frac{NT}{\log d}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{\mathrm{min}}((NT)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J})-\psi_{\mathrm{min}}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x,J,J})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|(NT)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x,J,J}\right|_{2}\leq s\left|(NT)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x,J,J}\right|_{\mathrm{max}}\leq\frac{\psi_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}}(J)}{2}.

where
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x,J,J}
is the sub-matrix of
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}
.

Therefore, conditional on the set
\mathcal{B}_{NT}=\left\{|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{x}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{x}|_{\mathrm{max}}\leq\frac{C_{0}}{16}\sqrt{\log d/(NT)}\right\}
, we have

\psi_{\mathrm{min}}((NT)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J})\geq\frac{\psi_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}}(J)}{2}

and thus
(NT)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J}
is nonsingular.

Given the invertibility of
(NT)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J}
, to prove
\sgn(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{w})=\sgn(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0})
, it suffices to show

\sgn\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{w,J}\right)=\sgn\left[\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,J}+\left((NT)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J}\right)^{-1}\left((NT)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{e}-\omega_{1}\vec{\mathbf{b}}\right)\right]=\sgn(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,J})

(A.1)

and for any
j\in J^{c}

\displaystyle\left|(NT)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J}\left((NT)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J}\right)^{-1}\left((NT)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{e}-\omega_{1}\vec{\mathbf{b}}\right)-\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{e}\right|

(A.2)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|-\omega_{1}\vec{g}_{j}|\leq\omega_{1}g_{j}.

Consider (
A.1
) first. It is sufficient to show
|(\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J})^{-1}(\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{e}-\omega_{1}\vec{\mathbf{b}})|_{\infty}<\beta_{\mathrm{min}}
.
Since
|(\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J})^{-1}|_{2}\leq\frac{2}{\psi_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}}(J)}
and
\beta_{\min}>\frac{\sqrt{s}\omega_{1}}{\psi_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}}(J)}(1+2\max_{j\in J}|g_{j}|)
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left(\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{e}-\omega_{1}\vec{\mathbf{b}}\right)\right|_{\infty}\leq\left|\left(\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J}\right)^{-1}\right|_{\infty}\left|\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{e}-\omega_{1}\vec{\mathbf{b}}\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2\sqrt{s}}{\psi_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}}(J)}\left(\left|\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{e}\right|_{\infty}+|\omega_{1}\vec{\mathbf{b}}|_{\infty}\right)\leq\frac{2\sqrt{s}}{\psi_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}}(J)}\left(\omega_{1}/2+\omega_{1}\max_{j\in J}|g_{j}|\right)<\beta_{\min}.

Next consider (
A.2
). By the triangle inequality, it suffices to show for any
j\in J^{c}

\left|\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J}(\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J})^{-1}(\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{e}-\omega_{1}\vec{\mathbf{b}})\right|+\left|\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{e}\right|\leq\omega_{1}g_{j}.

The first term is bounded by

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J}(\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J})^{-1}(\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{e}-\omega_{1}\vec{\mathbf{b}})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J}(\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J})^{-1}\right|_{1}\times\left(\left|\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{e}\right|_{\infty}+|\omega_{1}\vec{\mathbf{b}}|_{\infty}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2s}{\psi_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}}(J)}\left|\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J}\right|_{\infty}\times\left(\left|\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{J}^{\top}\mathbf{e}\right|_{\infty}+|\omega_{1}\vec{\mathbf{b}}|_{\infty}\right).

Since
\max_{j\in J^{c}}|\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{J}|_{\infty}\leq|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}|_{\mathrm{max}}+\frac{C_{0}}{16}\sqrt{\log d/(NT)}
by Lemma
A.5
(1), the left hand of (
A.2
) is bounded by

\left(\frac{2s|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}|_{\mathrm{max}}}{\psi_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}}(J)}+\frac{\psi_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}}(J)}{16}\right)\left(\frac{\omega_{1}}{2}+\omega_{1}\max_{j\in J}|g_{j}|\right).

The proof is completed given

\min_{j\in J^{c}}|g_{j}|\geq\left(\frac{2s|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}|_{\mathrm{max}}}{\psi_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}}(J)}+\frac{\psi_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}}(J)}{16}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}+\max_{j\in J}|g_{j}|\right).

∎

Proof of Theorem
2.1
.

(1).
Due to the property of convex optimization,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a solution if and only if there exists a subgradient

\vec{\mathbf{g}}\in\partial\sum_{j=1}^{d}|\widehat{\beta}_{j}|=\left\{\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\mid z_{j}=\sgn(\widehat{\beta}_{j})\ \text{for}\ \widehat{\beta}_{j}\neq 0\ \text{and}\ |z_{j}|\leq 1\ \text{elsewhere}\right\}

such that
|\vec{\mathbf{g}}|_{\infty}\leq 1
and
\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}+\omega_{1}\vec{\mathbf{g}}=\mathbf{0}
.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{NT}\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{bc}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{x}\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{e}+\sqrt{NT}\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{x}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{x}\right)(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0})

\displaystyle\eqqcolon

\displaystyle J_{1}+J_{2}.

Here,
J_{2}
is the error resulting from using an approximate inverse of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{x}
, which will be proved asymptotically negligible. In addition, the bias term
\omega_{1}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}\vec{\mathbf{g}}
resulting from the penalization of the parameters is known to be equal to
\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{X}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\right)
.

Hence, we have

\sqrt{NT}\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{bc}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}\right)=\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{e}+\sqrt{NT}\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{x}\right)(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}).

We will then provide: (1) a central limit theorem for
J_{1}
; and (2) a verification of asymptotic negligibility of
J_{2}
.

Consider
J_{2}
first. By
Yuan (2010)
, we have

\Omega_{x,j,j}=[{\Sigma}_{x,j,j}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x,j,-j}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x,-j,-j}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x,-j,j}]^{-1}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{and}\hskip 9.24994pt\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{x,j,-j}=-\Omega_{x,j,j}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x,j,-j}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x,-j,-j}^{-1},

where
\Omega_{x,j,j}
denotes the
j^{th}
diagonal entry of
\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{x}
,
\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{x,j,-j}
is the
1\times(d-1)
vector obtained by removing the
j^{th}
entry of the
j^{th}
row of
\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{x}
,
\Sigma_{x,j,j}
is the
j^{th}
diagonal entry of
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}
,
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x,-j,-j}
is the submatrix of
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}
with the
j^{th}
row and column removed,
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x,j,-j}
is the
j^{th}
row of
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}
with its
j^{th}
entry removed,
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x,-j,j}
is the
j^{th}
column of
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x}
with its
j^{th}
entry removed. Define the
(d-1)\times 1
vector

\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}=\argmin_{\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\frac{1}{NT}E|\mathbf{X}_{j}-\mathbf{X}_{-j}\mathbf{b}|_{2}^{2}

such that

\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}=\left(\frac{1}{NT}E(\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j})\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{NT}E(\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j})=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x,-j,-j}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x,-j,j}

and
|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}|_{2}\leq|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x,-j,-j}^{-1}|_{2}|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x,-j,j}|_{2}<\infty
.
Define
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j}=\mathbf{X}_{j}-\mathbf{X}_{-j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}
and thus
E(\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j})=\mathbf{0}
. Define
\tau_{j}^{2}=\frac{1}{NT}E|\mathbf{X}_{j}-\mathbf{X}_{-j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}|_{2}^{2}={\Sigma}_{x,j,j}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x,j,-j}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x,-j,-j}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x,-j,j}=1/\Omega_{x,j,j}
. Observe that
\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{x,j,-j}=-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}^{\top}/\tau_{j}^{2}
, and thus we can write
\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{x}=\mathbf{T}^{-2}\mathbf{C}
, where
\mathbf{T}
and
\mathbf{C}
is defined similarly to
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}^{-1}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}
but with
\tau_{j}^{2}
and
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}
replacing
\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}
and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{j}
.

Let
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{x,j}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{x,j}
denote the
j^{th}
row of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{x}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{x}
, respectively, and thus
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{x,j}=\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{-2}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}
. By the properties of Karush–Kuhn–Tucker (KKT) condition, we have

|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{x}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{x,j}^{\top}-\mathbf{e}_{j}|_{\infty}\leq\lambda_{j}/\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}=O_{P}(\sqrt{\log d/(NT)}),

where
\mathbf{e}_{j}
is the
j^{th}
unit column vector. Hence, by using Lemma
A.5
(3), we have

\displaystyle|J_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{\rho}|_{1}\times|\sqrt{NT}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0})|_{1}\times\max_{j\in H}|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{x}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{x,j}^{\top}-\mathbf{e}_{j}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}(s\sqrt{\log d})O_{P}(\sqrt{\log d/(NT)})=o_{P}(1).

Next, consider
J_{1}
. Write

J_{1}=\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\top}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{x}-\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{x})\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{e}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{x}\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{e}\eqqcolon J_{1,1}+J_{1,2}.

For
J_{1,1}
, by using Lemma
A.5
(3), we have

\displaystyle|J_{1,1}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\sum_{j\in H}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{x,j}-\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{x,j})\rho_{j}|_{1}\times|\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{e}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}(\sum_{j\in H}s_{j}\sqrt{\log d/(NT)})O_{P}(\sqrt{\log d})=o_{P}(1).

To complete the proof, it is sufficient to show that
J_{1,1}\to_{D}N(0,1)
.

We next prove the asymptotic normality by using martingale apprxoimation technique and the martingale central limit theorem. Define
S_{j,NT}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{u}_{j,t}
and
\overline{u}_{j,t}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}X_{j,it}e_{it}
. Hence,
J_{1,1}=\sum_{k\in H}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\rho_{k}\Omega_{x,k,j}S_{j,NT}
.

Let
S_{j,NT,L}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{l=0}^{L-1}\mathcal{P}_{t-l}(\overline{u}_{j,t})
and
\widehat{S}_{j,NT,L}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{l=0}^{L-1}\mathcal{P}_{t}(\overline{u}_{j,t+l})
, in which
\mathcal{P}_{t}(\cdot)=E[\cdot\mid\mathcal{F}_{t}]-E[\cdot\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]
,
L\to\infty
and
L/T\to 0
. Note that
\overline{u}_{j,t}=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{P}_{t-l}(\overline{u}_{j,t})
and
\{\mathcal{P}_{t-l}(\overline{u}_{j,t})\}_{t=1}^{T}
is a sequence of martingale differences, and thus

\displaystyle\|S_{j,NT,L}-S_{j,NT}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{l=L}^{\infty}\mathcal{P}_{t-l}(\overline{u}_{j,t})\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{l=L}^{\infty}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathcal{P}_{t-l}(\overline{u}_{j,t})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{l=L}^{\infty}\left\{\sum_{t=1}^{T}E\left[\left(\mathcal{P}_{t-l}(\overline{u}_{j.t})\right)^{2}\right]\right\}^{1/2}\leq O(1)\sum_{l=L}^{\infty}\delta_{2}(u_{j},l)\to 0,

where the second inequality follows from Burkholder inequality, and the last step follows from
L\to\infty
. Similarly, by using Burkholder inequality, we have as
L\to\infty
and
L/T\to 0

\displaystyle\|\widehat{S}_{j,NT,L}-S_{j,NT,L}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{l=0}^{L-1}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{l}\mathcal{P}_{t-l}(\overline{u}_{j,t})\right\|_{2}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{l=0}^{L-1}\left\|\sum_{t=T-l+1}^{T}\mathcal{P}_{t}(\overline{u}_{j,t+l})\right\|_{2}\to 0.

Hence, we have
\|\widehat{S}_{j,NT,L}-S_{j,NT}\|_{2}\to 0
. Note that
\{\sum_{l=0}^{L-1}\mathcal{P}_{t}(\overline{u}_{j,t+l})\}_{t=1}^{T}
is a sequence of martingale differences subject to
\mathscr{F}_{t}
, so the asymptotic normality can be easily obtained by using a standard martingale central limit theory.

The proof is now completed.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.2
.

Write

\displaystyle T_{u}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{\ell})-\boldsymbol{\Theta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(T_{u}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{\ell})-E(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{\ell})\right)+\left(E(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{\ell})-\boldsymbol{\Theta}\right)\eqqcolon J_{3}+J_{4},

where
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{\ell}=\left(\Theta_{\ell,kl}\right)_{k,l\leq d}=\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}a((t-s)/\ell)\mathbf{x}_{it}\mathbf{x}_{js}^{\top}e_{it}e_{js}
.

Consider the bias term
J_{4}
first. Note that
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{k}=E(\overline{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\overline{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{\top})
and thus

\displaystyle J_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=-\ell}^{\ell}[a(k/\ell)-1]\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{k}-\sum_{k=-\ell}^{\ell}\frac{k}{T}[a(k/\ell)-1]\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{k}-\sum_{k=\ell+1}^{T-1}\frac{T-k}{k}\left(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{k}+\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{k}^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle\eqqcolon

\displaystyle J_{4,1}+J_{4,2}+J_{4,3}.

For
J_{4,1}
, by Assumption
4
(1), for
\forall\epsilon>0
, we choose
\nu_{\epsilon}>0
such that

\displaystyle|k/\ell|<\nu_{\epsilon}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{and}\hskip 9.24994pt\left|\frac{1-a(k/\ell)}{|k/\ell|^{q_{a}}}-C_{q}\right|<\epsilon.

Letting
\ell_{T}^{*}=\lfloor\nu_{\epsilon}\ell\rfloor
, write

\displaystyle\ell^{q_{a}}\times J_{4,1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=-\ell_{T}^{*}}^{\ell_{T}^{*}}\frac{a\left(k/\ell\right)-1}{\left|k/\ell\right|^{q_{a}}}|k|^{q_{a}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{k}+\sum_{k=\ell_{T}^{*}+1}^{\ell}\frac{a\left(k/\ell\right)-1}{\left|k/\ell\right|^{q}}|k|^{q}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{k}+\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{k}^{\top}).

Since
|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{k}|_{2}=O(k^{-(q_{a}+\epsilon)})
for some
\epsilon>1
, the first term on the RHS converges to
-C_{q}\sum_{k=-\infty}^{\infty}|k|^{q_{a}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{k}
in spectral norm. For the second term, since
|a(\cdot)|\leq M
,
\left|\frac{1-a(k/\ell)}{|k/\ell|^{q_{a}}}\right|\leq(M+1)/\nu_{\epsilon}^{q_{a}}
due to the fact that
k/\ell\geq\nu_{\epsilon}
. Then this term in spectral norm is bounded by

\displaystyle(M+1)/\nu_{\epsilon}^{q_{a}}\sum_{k=\ell_{T}^{*}+1}^{\infty}k^{2}|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{k}+\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{k}^{\top}|_{2}\to 0.

Therefore,
|J_{4,1}|_{2}=O(\ell^{-q_{a}})
. Similarly, it can be shown that
|J_{4,2}|_{2}=o(\ell^{-q_{a}})
and
|J_{4,3}|_{2}=o(\ell^{-q_{a}})
, which implies that
|J_{4}|_{2}=O(\ell^{-q_{a}})
.

Now consider
J_{3}
. We first prove
\max_{1\leq k,l\leq d}|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}-\Theta_{\ell,kl}|=O_{P}(\sqrt{\ell\log d/T})
. Write

\displaystyle\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}-\Theta_{\ell,kl}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}a((t-s)/\ell)\left(\widehat{\overline{u}}_{k,t}\widehat{\overline{u}}_{l,s}-\overline{u}_{k,t}\overline{u}_{l,s}\right)+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}a((t-s)/\ell)\left(\overline{u}_{k,t}\overline{u}_{l,s}-E(\overline{u}_{k,t}\overline{u}_{l,s})\right)

\displaystyle\eqqcolon

\displaystyle J_{5}+J_{6}.

By using Lemma
A.8
(1), we have
\max_{1\leq k,l\leq d}|J_{6}|=O_{P}(\sqrt{\ell\log d/T})
. For
J_{5}
, write

\displaystyle J_{5}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}a((t-s)/\ell)\left(\widehat{\overline{u}}_{k,t}-\overline{u}_{k,t}\right)\left(\widehat{\overline{u}}_{l,s}-\overline{u}_{l,s}\right)+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}a((t-s)/\ell)\left(\widehat{\overline{u}}_{k,t}-\overline{u}_{k,t}\right)\overline{u}_{l,s}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}a((t-s)/\ell)\overline{u}_{k,s}\left(\widehat{\overline{u}}_{l,t}-\overline{u}_{l,t}\right).

Applying Lemmas
A.8
(2)-(3) to the above three terms, we have
J_{5}=o_{P}(\sqrt{\ell\log d/T})
. Hence, we have proved
\max_{1\leq k,l\leq d}|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}-\Theta_{\ell,kl}|=O_{P}(\sqrt{\ell\log d/T})
. Next, write

J_{3}=\left|T_{u}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{\ell})-E(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{\ell})\right|_{2}\leq\left|T_{u}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{\ell})-T_{u}(E(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{\ell}))\right|_{2}+\left|T_{u}(E(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{\ell}))-E(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{\ell})\right|_{2}\eqqcolon J_{3,1}+J_{3,2}.

The term
J_{3,2}
is bounded by

\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\sum_{l=1}^{d}|\Theta_{\ell,kl}|\mathbb{I}(|\Theta_{\ell,kl}|\leq u)\leq u^{1-p}C(d)=O\left((\ell\log d/T)^{(1-p)/2}C(d)\right).

For
J_{3,1}
,

\displaystyle J_{3,1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\sum_{l=1}^{d}|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}|\mathbb{I}(|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}|\geq u,|\Theta_{\ell,kl}|<u)+\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\sum_{l=1}^{d}|\Theta_{\ell,kl}|\mathbb{I}(|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}|<u,|\Theta_{\ell,kl}|\geq u)

\displaystyle+\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\sum_{l=1}^{d}|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}-{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}|\mathbb{I}(|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}|\geq u,|\Theta_{\ell,kl}|\geq u)\eqqcolon J_{3,11}+J_{3,12}+J_{3,13}.

For
J_{3,13}
, by using
\max_{1\leq k,l\leq d}|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}-\Theta_{\ell,kl}|=O_{P}(\sqrt{\ell\log d/T})
, we have

J_{3,13}\leq\max_{1\leq k,l\leq d}|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}-\Theta_{\ell,kl}|\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\sum_{l=1}^{d}|\Theta_{\ell,kl}|^{p}u^{-p}=O_{P}\left((\ell\log d/T)^{(1-p)/2}C(d)\right).

For
J_{3,11}
, write

\displaystyle J_{3,11}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\sum_{l=1}^{d}|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}-{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}|\mathbb{I}(|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}|\geq u,|\Theta_{\ell,kl}|<u)+\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\sum_{l=1}^{d}|{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}|\mathbb{I}(|\Theta_{\ell,kl}|<u)

\displaystyle\eqqcolon

\displaystyle J_{3,111}+J_{3,112}.

For
J_{3,112}
, we have
J_{3,112}\leq u^{1-p}C(d).
For
J_{3,111}
, take some
a\in(0,1)
, then

\displaystyle J_{3,112}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\sum_{l=1}^{d}|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}-{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}|\mathbb{I}(|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}|\geq u,|\Theta_{\ell,kl}|<au)

\displaystyle+\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\sum_{l=1}^{d}|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}-{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}|\mathbb{I}(|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}|\geq u,au\leq|\Theta_{\ell,kl}|<u)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k,l\leq d}|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}-{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}|\times\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\mathbb{I}(|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}-\Theta_{\ell,kl}|>(1-a)u)

\displaystyle+\max_{1\leq k,l\leq d}|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}-{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}|\times C(d)(au)^{1-p}.

Note that since
\max_{1\leq k,l\leq d}|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}-\Theta_{\ell,kl}|=O_{P}(\sqrt{\ell\log d/T})
, then for some large
C>0
and
u=C\sqrt{\ell\log d/T}
, we have

\Pr\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\mathbb{I}(|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}-\Theta_{\ell,kl}|>au)>0\right)=\Pr\left(\max_{1\leq k,l\leq d}|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}-{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}|>(1-a)u\right)\to 0.

Combining the above analysis, we have
J_{3,11}=O_{P}\left((\ell\log d/T)^{(1-p)/2}C(d)\right)
.

For
J_{3,12}
, we have

\displaystyle J_{3,12}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left(|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}-\Theta_{\ell,kl}|+|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}|\right)\mathbb{I}(|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}|<u,|\Theta_{\ell,kl}|\geq u)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k,l\leq d}|\widehat{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}-{\Theta}_{\ell,kl}|\times\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\mathbb{I}(|\Theta_{\ell,kl}|\geq u)+u\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\mathbb{I}(|\Theta_{\ell,kl}|\geq u)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}\left((\ell\log d/T)^{(1-p)/2}C(d)\right).

Combining the above results, the proof is now completed.
∎

In this file, we provide two appendices. Specifically, in Appendix B, Appendix
B.1
presents the proofs of technical lemmas collected in Appendix
A.4
. Appendix
B.2
provide the full proofs of the main results listed in Section
3
. Appendix C provides some technical lemmas, which are used to prove the results of Section
3
and the asymptotic properties of the oracle least squares estimator for the HD panel models with interactive fixed effects.

Appendix B

B.1
Proofs of the Technical Lemmas

Proof of Lemma
A.3
.

(1). We apply the martingale approximation technique and approximate
x_{j,it}e_{it}
by
m
-dependent processes. Then we prove this lemma by using delicate block techniques and the results on independent random variables.

For
t\geq 1
and
m\geq 0
, define
S_{j,Nt,m}\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{k=1}^{t}u_{j,ik,m}
and
u_{j,ik,m}\coloneqq E\left[x_{j,ik}e_{ik}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k,k-m}\right]
, where
\mathcal{F}_{k,k-m}=(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{k},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{k-1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{k-m})
. Therefore,
u_{j,ik,m}
and
u_{j,ik^{\prime},m}
are independent if
|k-k^{\prime}|>m
. Write

u_{j,it}=u_{j,it}-u_{j,it,T}+\sum_{m=1}^{T}(u_{j,it,m}-u_{j,it,m-1})+u_{j,it,0},

which follows that

\displaystyle\|\max_{1\leq t\leq T}|S_{j,Nt}|\|_{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\max_{1\leq t\leq T}|S_{j,Nt}-S_{j,Nt,T}|\|_{q}+\sum_{m=1}^{T}\|\max_{1\leq t\leq T}|S_{j,Nt,m}-S_{j,Nt,m-1}|\|_{q}

\displaystyle+\|\max_{1\leq t\leq T}|S_{j,Nt,0}|\|_{q}

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}.

Consider
I_{2}
first. By the stationarity of
\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}u_{j,ik,m}\}_{k=1}^{T}
, we have

\|\max_{1\leq t\leq T}|S_{j,Nt,m}-S_{j,Nt,m-1}|\|_{q}=\|\max_{0\leq t\leq T-1}|\sum_{k=T-t}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(u_{j,ik,m}-u_{j,ik,m-1})|\|_{q}.

Note that
\{u_{j,i(T-k),m}-u_{j,i(T-k),m-1}\}_{k=0}^{T-1}
are martingale differences, and thus

\left\{\left|\sum_{k=T-t}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(u_{j,ik,m}-u_{j,ik,m-1})\right|\right\}_{t=0}^{T-1}

is a positive submartingale with respect to
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{T-t-m},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{T-t-m+1},\ldots)
. Therefore, by Doob
L_{p}
maximal inequality, we have

\|\max_{1\leq t\leq T}|S_{j,Nt,m}-S_{j,Nt,m-1}|\|_{q}\leq q/(q-1)\|S_{j,NT,m}-S_{j,NT,m-1}\|_{q}.

Define
Y_{l,m}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{k=1+(l-1)m}^{\min(lm,T)}(u_{j,ik,m}-u_{j,ik,m-1})
and
b=\lfloor T/m\rfloor+1
, we have

|S_{j,NT,m}-S_{j,NT,m-1}|=\left|\sum_{l=1}^{b}Y_{l,m}\right|.

Note that
Y_{1,m},Y_{3,m},\ldots
are independent and
Y_{2,m},Y_{4,m},\ldots
are also independent, then by Rosenthal inequality for independent variables (e.g.,
Johnson, 1985
),

\|\sum_{l\text{ is odd}}Y_{l,m}\|_{q}\leq\frac{14.5q}{\log q}\left(\|\sum_{l\text{ is odd}}Y_{l,m}\|_{2}+\left(\sum_{l\text{ is odd}}\|Y_{l,m}\|_{q}^{q}\right)^{1/q}\right),

for
q\geq 2
we have

\displaystyle\|S_{j,NT,m}-S_{j,NT,m-1}\|_{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{14.5q}{\log q}\left[\|\sum_{l\text{ is odd}}Y_{l,m}\|_{2}+\|\sum_{l\text{ is even}}Y_{l,m}\|_{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left(\sum_{l\text{ is odd}}\|Y_{l,m}\|_{q}^{q}\right)^{1/q}+\left(\sum_{l\text{ is even}}\|Y_{l,m}\|_{q}^{q}\right)^{1/q}\right].

In addition, by Burkholder inequality for martingale differences, we have

\|Y_{l,m}\|_{q}^{2}\leq(q-1)\sum_{k=1+(l-1)m}^{\min(lm,T)}\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}(u_{j,ik,m}-u_{j,ik,m-1})\|_{q}^{2}.

For
\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}(u_{j,ik,m}-u_{j,ik,m-1})\|_{q}
, by Assumption
1
we have

\displaystyle\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}(u_{j,ik,m}-u_{j,ik,m-1})\|_{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}E\left(u_{j,i}(\mathcal{F}_{k})\mid\mathcal{F}_{k,k-m}\right)-E\left(u_{j,i}(\mathcal{F}_{k})\mid\mathcal{F}_{k,k-m+1}\right)\|_{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}E\left(u_{j,i}(\mathcal{F}_{m})\mid\mathcal{F}_{m,0}\right)-E\left(u_{j,i}(\mathcal{F}_{m})\mid\mathcal{F}_{m,1}\right)\|_{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}E\left(u_{j,i}(\mathcal{F}_{m})\mid\mathcal{F}_{m,0}\right)-E\left(u_{j,i}(\mathcal{F}_{m}^{*})\mid\mathcal{F}_{m,0}\right)\|_{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{N}\delta_{q}(u_{j},m).

Hence, for
1\leq l\leq b
we have

\|Y_{l,m}\|_{q}\leq\sqrt{(q-1)(\min(lm,T)-(l-1)m)}\sqrt{N}\delta_{q}(u_{j},m).

Then, for
1\leq m\leq T
, we have

\|S_{j,NT,m}-S_{j,NT,m-1}\|_{q}\leq\frac{29q}{\log q}\left(\sqrt{NT}\delta_{2}(u_{j},m)+\sqrt{N}\sqrt{q-1}T^{1/q}m^{1/2-1/q}\delta_{q}(u_{j},m)\right).

By the above developments and
q/(q-1)\leq 2
when
q\geq 2
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{T}\|\max_{1\leq t\leq T}|S_{j,Nt,m}-S_{j,Nt,m-1}|\|_{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{87q}{\log q}\left(\sqrt{NT}\sum_{m=1}^{T}\delta_{2}(u_{j},m)+\sqrt{N}\sqrt{q-1}T^{1/q}\sum_{m=1}^{T}m^{1/2-1/q}\delta_{q}(u_{j},m)\right).

Similar to the above developments, for
I_{1}
, we have

\|\max_{1\leq t\leq T}|S_{j,Nt}-S_{j,Nt,T}|\|_{q}\leq 3\sqrt{q-1}\sqrt{NT}\sum_{m=T+1}^{\infty}\delta_{q}(u_{j},m).

For
I_{3}
, note that
\{u_{j,it,0}\}
are independent random variables, by Doob
L_{p}
maximal inequality and Rosenthal inequality for independent variables, we have

\displaystyle\|\max_{1\leq t\leq T}|S_{j,Nt,0}|\|_{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle q/(q-1)\|S_{j,NT,0}\|_{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle q/(q-1)\frac{14.5q}{\log q}\left(\sqrt{T}\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}u_{j,it,0}\|_{2}+T^{1/q}\left(\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}u_{j,it,0}\|_{q}^{q}\right)^{1/q}\right).

Note that
u_{j,it,0}=E(u_{j,i}(\mathcal{F}_{t})\mid\mathcal{F}_{t,t})=_{D}E(u_{j,i}(\mathcal{F}_{0})\mid\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0})
and
E(u_{j,i}(\mathcal{F}_{0}^{*})\mid\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0})=0
, so we have

\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}u_{j,it,0}\|_{q}\leq\sqrt{N}\delta_{q}(u_{j},0).

Hence,
\|\max_{1\leq t\leq T}|S_{j,Nt,0}|\|_{q}\leq\frac{29}{\log q}\left(\sqrt{NT}\delta_{q}(u_{j},0)+T^{1/q}\sqrt{N}\delta_{q}(u_{j},0)\right)
.

Combining the above analysis, we have

\displaystyle\|\max_{1\leq t\leq T}|S_{j,Nt}|\|_{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{NT}\left[\frac{87q}{\log q}\sum_{m=1}^{T}\delta_{2}(u_{j},m)+3\sqrt{q-1}\sum_{m=T+1}^{\infty}\delta_{q}(u_{j},m)+\frac{29q}{\log q}\delta_{2}(u_{j},0)\right]

\displaystyle+\sqrt{N}T^{1/q}\left[\frac{87q\sqrt{q-1}}{\log q}\sum_{m=1}^{T}m^{1/2-1/q}\delta_{q}(u_{j},m)+\frac{29q}{\log q}\delta_{q}(u_{j},0)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{q}\sqrt{NT}\Delta_{2}(u_{j},0)+c_{q}\sqrt{N}T^{1/q}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}(m\wedge T)^{1/2-1/q}\delta_{q}(u_{j},m).

The proof of part (1) is now complete.

(2). The proof is similar with that of part (1), so it is omitted here.
∎

Proof of Lemma
A.4
.

The proof of Lemma
A.4
is similar with that of Lemma
1
(1), so it is omitted here.
∎

Proof of Lemma
A.5
.

(1). Note that

|\mathbf{v}|_{1}=|\mathbf{v}_{\mathbf{J}}|_{1}+|\mathbf{v}_{\mathbf{J}^{c}}|_{1}\leq 4|\mathbf{v}_{\mathbf{J}}|_{1}\leq 4\sqrt{s}|\mathbf{v}_{\mathbf{J}}|_{2},

which follows that

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{x}\mathbf{v}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{x}\mathbf{v}-|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{x}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{x}|_{\mathrm{max}}|\mathbf{v}|_{1}^{2}\geq\mathbf{v}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{x}\mathbf{v}-16s|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{x}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{x}|_{\mathrm{max}}|\mathbf{v}|_{2}^{2}.

Using Lemma
A.4
with
x=\frac{C_{0}}{16}\sqrt{\log d/(NT)}
for some constant
C_{0}>0
,
\mathcal{B}_{NT}=\{|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{x}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{x}|_{\mathrm{max}}\leq x\}
holds with probability larger than
1-C_{1}(\frac{dT^{1-q/2}}{(\log d)^{q/2}}+d^{-C_{2}})
for some
C_{1},C_{2}>0
.

(2). Given the condition
|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}|_{1}<\infty
, we first show the weak dependence properties of
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j}
. By the construction of
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j}
, we have
\eta_{j,it}=x_{j,it}-\mathbf{x}_{-j,it}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}
, where
\mathbf{x}_{-j,it}
is the sub-vector of
\mathbf{x}_{it}
with the
j^{th}
column removed. Then, by using Assumption
2
.1 and
|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}|_{1}<\infty
, for any
j^{\prime}\neq j
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(x_{j^{\prime},it}\eta_{j,it}-x_{j^{\prime},it}^{*}\eta_{j,it}^{*})\right\|_{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(x_{j^{\prime},it}x_{j,it}-x_{j^{\prime},it}^{*}x_{j,it}^{*})\right\|_{q}+\sum_{l=1,\neq j}^{d}\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(x_{j^{\prime},it}x_{l,it}-x_{j^{\prime},it}^{*}x_{l,it}^{*})\right\|_{q}|\lambda_{j,l}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(x_{j^{\prime},it}x_{j,it}-x_{j^{\prime},it}^{*}x_{j,it}^{*})\right\|_{q}(1+|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}|_{1})=O(t^{-\alpha}).

Given the weak dependence properties of
x_{j^{\prime},it}\eta_{j,it}
and
\widetilde{\omega}_{j}\asymp\sqrt{\log d/(NT)}
, the proof of part (2) is then similar with that of Lemma
2
, so it is omitted here.

(3). The Karush-Kuhn-Tucker (KKT) condition of the optimization problem (
2.5
) implies that there exists a subgradient
\vec{\mathbf{k}}_{j}
such that

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{X}_{j}-\mathbf{X}_{-j}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{j}\right)+\widetilde{\omega}_{j}\vec{\mathbf{k}}_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{0}

\displaystyle-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{j}^{\top}\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{X}_{j}-\mathbf{X}_{-j}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{j}\right)+\widetilde{\omega}_{j}|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{j}|_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{0}

which implies that

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathbf{X}_{j}-\mathbf{X}_{-j}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{j})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}/(NT)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j}}{NT}+\frac{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}}{NT}-\frac{(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}}{NT}-\frac{(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j}}{NT}.

Hence, for all
j\in[d]
, we have

\displaystyle|\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}-\tau_{j}^{2}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j}}{NT}-\tau_{j}^{2}\right|+\left|\frac{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}}{NT}\right|+\left|\frac{(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}}{NT}\right|+\left|\frac{(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j}}{NT}\right|

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle I_{4}+I_{5}+I_{6}+I_{7}.

For
I_{4}
, it can be shown that
|\frac{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j}}{NT}-\tau_{j}^{2}|=O_{P}((NT)^{-1/2})
by using similar arguments as the proof of Lemma
A.6
(1). For
I_{5}
, by part (2) of this lemma, we have

I_{5}\leq|\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}/(NT)|_{\infty}|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}|_{1}\leq\sqrt{s_{j}}|\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}/(NT)|_{\infty}|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}|_{2}=O_{P}(\sqrt{s_{j}\log d/(NT)}).

Consider
I_{6}
. Note that by the KKT condition, we have

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{X}_{j}-\mathbf{X}_{-j}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{j}\right)+\lambda_{j}\vec{\mathbf{k}}_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{0}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{X}_{-j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}+\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j}-\mathbf{X}_{-j}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{j}\right)+\lambda_{j}\vec{\mathbf{k}}_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{0}.

Hence, by using part (2) of this lemma, we have

\left|\frac{(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}}{NT}\right|_{\infty}=|\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{j}/(NT)+\lambda_{j}\vec{\mathbf{k}}_{j}|_{\infty}=O_{P}(\sqrt{\log d/(NT)})

and

I_{6}\leq\left|\frac{(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}}{NT}\right|_{\infty}|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}|_{1}=O_{P}(\sqrt{s_{j}\log d/(NT)}).

Similarly,
I_{7}=O_{P}(s_{j}\log d/(NT))
. Therefore,
|\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}-\tau_{j}^{2}|=O_{P}(\sqrt{s_{j}\log d/(NT)})
.

Note that
\tau_{j}^{2}=1/\Omega_{x,j,j}\geq 1/\psi_{\mathrm{max}}(\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{x})=\psi_{\mathrm{min}}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{x})>0
, and thus
\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}\geq\tau_{j}^{2}-|\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}-\tau_{j}^{2}|>0
with probability approaching to 1 as
|\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}-\tau_{j}^{2}|=O_{P}(\sqrt{s_{j}\log d/(NT)})
, which also implies that

\left|\frac{1}{\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\tau_{j}^{2}}\right|=\frac{|\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}-\tau_{j}^{2}|}{\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}\tau_{j}^{2}}=O_{P}(\sqrt{s_{j}\log d/(NT)}).

Next, consider
|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{x,j}-\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{x,j}|_{1}
. As
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{x,j}=\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}/\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}
, by using part (2) of this lemma and
|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}|_{1}=O(\sqrt{s_{j}})
, we have

\displaystyle|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{x,j}-\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{x,j}|_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}/\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}-\mathbf{C}_{j}/\tau_{j}^{2}|_{1}\leq|1/\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}-1/\tau_{j}^{2}|+|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{j}/\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}/\tau_{j}^{2}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|1/\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}-1/\tau_{j}^{2}|+|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{j}/\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}/\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}|_{1}+|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}/\widehat{\tau}_{j}^{2}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}/\tau_{j}^{2}|_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}(\sqrt{s_{j}\log d/(NT)})+O_{P}(s_{j}\sqrt{\log d/(NT)})+O_{P}(s_{j}\sqrt{\log d/(NT)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}(s_{j}\sqrt{\log d/(NT)}).

Similarly, by using
|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}|_{2}=O(1)
, we have
|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{x,j}-\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{x,j}|_{2}=O_{P}(\sqrt{s_{j}\log d/(NT)})
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
A.6
.

(1). Consider
\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}b_{t}(\overline{u}_{j,t}-\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t})\|_{q}
first. Define
D_{t,k}=E[\overline{u}_{j,t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-k,t}]-E[\overline{u}_{j,t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-k+1,t}]
, then
\{D_{t,k},\ t=T,\ldots,1\}
form martingale differences with respect to
\mathcal{F}_{t-k,\infty}
. In addition, by Jensen inequality, we have
\|D_{t,k}\|_{q}\leq\delta_{q}(u_{j},k)
.

By using Burkholder inequality and Minkowski inequality, for
q\geq 2
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}b_{t}D_{t,k}\right\|_{q}^{2}\leq O(1)\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|b_{t}D_{t,k}\|_{q}^{2}=O(1)\sum_{t=1}^{T}b_{t}^{2}\delta_{q}^{2}(u_{j},k).

Since
\overline{u}_{j,t}-\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t}=\sum_{k=m+1}^{\infty}D_{t,k}
, the result follows.

The proof of
\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}b_{t}\overline{u}_{j,t}\|_{q}
is similar to above, so omitted here.

(2). In what follows, let

\displaystyle Z_{j,t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t}a_{t+1-s}\overline{u}_{j,s},\hskip 9.24994pt\widetilde{Z}_{j^{\prime},t}=\sum_{s=1}^{t}a_{t+1-s}\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},s},

\displaystyle L_{T,j,j^{\prime}}^{\diamond}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq s<t\leq T}a_{t-s}\overline{u}_{j,t}\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},s}=\sum_{t=2}^{T}\overline{u}_{j,t}\widetilde{Z}_{j^{\prime},t-1}.

Also, let
Z_{j,t,\{k\}},\overline{u}_{j,t,\{k\}}
be the coupled version of
Z_{t}
and
x_{t}
replacing
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{k}
with
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{k}^{\prime}
.

Define the projection operator
\mathcal{P}_{t}(\cdot)=E[\cdot\mid\mathcal{F}_{t}]-E[\cdot\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]
. By Jensen inequality,

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{P}_{k}(L_{T,j,j^{\prime}}-L_{T,j,j^{\prime}}^{\diamond})\|_{q/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|E\left[\sum_{t=2}^{T}[\overline{u}_{j,t}(Z_{j^{\prime},t-1}-\widetilde{Z}_{j^{\prime},t-1})\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}\right]-E\left[\sum_{t=2}^{T}[\overline{u}_{j,t,\{k\}}(Z_{j^{\prime},t-1,\{k\}}-\widetilde{Z}_{j^{\prime},t-1,\{k\}})\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}\right]\right\|_{q/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{t=2}^{T}\overline{u}_{j,t,\{k\}}(Z_{j^{\prime},t-1}-\widetilde{Z}_{j^{\prime},t-1}-Z_{j^{\prime},t-1,\{k\}}+\widetilde{Z}_{j^{\prime},t-1,\{k\}})\right\|_{q/2}

\displaystyle+\left\|\sum_{t=2}^{T}[\overline{u}_{j,t}-\overline{u}_{j,t,\{k\}}](Z_{j^{\prime},t-1}-\widetilde{Z}_{j^{\prime},t-1})\right\|_{q/2}

\displaystyle\eqqcolon

\displaystyle I_{8}+I_{9}.

We consider
I_{8}
first. Since
\|\overline{u}_{j^{\prime},t}-\overline{u}_{j^{\prime},t,\{k\}}\|_{q}\leq\delta_{q}(u_{j^{\prime}},t-k)
and
\|\overline{u}_{j^{\prime},t}-\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},t}\|_{q}\leq\Psi_{q}(u_{j}^{\prime},m+1)
, we thus obtain that

\displaystyle\|\overline{u}_{j^{\prime},t}-\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},t}-\overline{u}_{j^{\prime},t,\{k\}}+\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},t,\{k\}}\|_{q}\leq 2\min\left(\delta_{q}(u_{j^{\prime}},t-k),\Psi_{q}(u_{j^{\prime}},m+1)\right).

In addition, by Minkowski inequality, Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and part (1) of this lemma, we have

\displaystyle I_{8}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{s=1}^{T-1}(\overline{u}_{j^{\prime},s}-\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},s}-\overline{u}_{j^{\prime},s,\{k\}}+\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},s,\{k\}})\sum_{t=s+1}^{T}a_{t-s}\overline{u}_{j,t,\{k\}}\right\|_{q/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{T-1}\left\|\overline{u}_{j^{\prime},s}-\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},s}-\overline{u}_{j^{\prime},s,\{k\}}+\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},s,\{k\}}\right\|_{q}\cdot\left\|\sum_{t=s+1}^{T}a_{t-s}\overline{u}_{j,t,\{k\}}\right\|_{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(A_{T})\sum_{s=1}^{T-1}\min\left(\delta_{q}(u_{j^{\prime}},s-k),\Psi_{q}(u_{j^{\prime}},m+1)\right).

We now consider
I_{9}
. By part (1) of this lemma, we have

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq t\leq T}\|Z_{j^{\prime},t-1}-\widetilde{Z}_{j^{\prime},t-1}\|_{4}\leq O(1)A_{T}\Delta_{q}(u_{j^{\prime}},m+1).

Also, note that
I_{9}
is actually
I_{9,k}
, and the sub-index
k
is suppressed previously for notational simplicity. In addition, by , we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k=-\infty}^{T}I_{9,k}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)\sum_{k=-\infty}^{T}A_{T}^{2}\Delta_{q}^{2}(u_{j^{\prime}},m+1)\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\delta_{q}(u_{j},t-k)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)A_{T}^{2}\Delta_{q}^{2}(u_{j^{\prime}},m+1)\sum_{k=-\infty}^{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\delta_{q}(u_{j},t-k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(1)TA_{T}^{2}\Delta_{q}^{2}(u_{j^{\prime}},m+1).

Similarly,
\sum_{k=-\infty}^{T}I_{8,k}^{2}=O(1)TA_{T}^{2}d_{j^{\prime},m,q}^{2}
. Since
\{\mathscr{P}_{k}(L_{T}-L_{T}^{\diamond})\}_{k}
is a sequence of martingale differences, by Burkholder inequality and
\Delta_{q}(u_{j^{\prime}},m+1)\leq d_{j^{\prime},m,q}
, we have

\displaystyle\|L_{T,j,j^{\prime}}-E(L_{T,j,j^{\prime}})-L_{T,j,j^{\prime}}^{\diamond}-E(L_{T,j,j^{\prime}}^{\diamond})\|_{q/2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{k=-\infty}^{T}\mathscr{P}_{k}(L_{T,j,j^{\prime}}-L_{T,j,j^{\prime}}^{\diamond})\right\|_{q/2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)\sum_{k=-\infty}^{T}\|\mathscr{P}_{k}(L_{T,j,j^{\prime}}-L_{T,j,j^{\prime}}^{\diamond})\|_{q/2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(1)TA_{T}^{2}d_{j^{\prime},m,q}^{2}.

Similarly, we have
\|\widetilde{L}_{T,j,j^{\prime}}-E(\widetilde{L}_{T,j,j^{\prime}})-L_{T,j,j^{\prime}}^{\diamond}-E(L_{T,j,j^{\prime}}^{\diamond})\|_{q/2}^{2}=O(1)TA_{T}^{2}d_{j,m,q}^{2}
.

The proof is now complete.

(3). The proof is similar to that of part (2), so omitted here.
∎

Proof of Lemma
A.7
.

(1). Let
m=\lfloor T^{\theta}\rfloor
for some
0<\theta<1
. By Lemmas
A.6
(2) and (3) and using Markov inequality, we have

\Pr(|L_{NT,j,j'} - E(L_{NT,j,j'}) - \widetilde{L}_{NT,j,j'} - E(\widetilde{L}_{NT,j,j'})|\geq x_T/2)\leq C_{q}x_{T}^{-q/2}T^{q/2-(\alpha-1)\theta q/2}.

Then to complete the proof, it is sufficient to show

\Pr(|\widetilde{L}_{NT,j,j'} - E(\widetilde{L}_{NT,j,j'})|\geq x_T/2)\leq C_{q,\delta,\theta}x_{T}^{-q/2}(T\log T)(m^{q/2-1-(\alpha-1)\theta q/2}+1).

since

\displaystyle\Pr(|L_{NT,j,j'} - E(L_{NT,j,j'})|\geq x_T)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Pr(|L_{NT,j,j'} - E(L_{NT,j,j'}) - \widetilde{L}_{NT,j,j'} - E(\widetilde{L}_{NT,j,j'})|\geq x_T/2)

\displaystyle+\Pr(| \widetilde{L}_{NT,j,j'} - E(\widetilde{L}_{NT,j,j'})|\geq x_T/2).

Let
z_{T}
satisfy
T^{1+\delta}/z_{T}\to 0
and
l_{T}=\lfloor-\log(\log T)/(\log\theta)\rfloor
such that
T^{\theta^{l_{T}}}\leq e
. Let
y_{T}=z_{T}/(2l_{T})
and
1<m<T/4
.

Define
\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{it,\theta}=E[\mathbf{u}_{it}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-\lfloor m^{\theta}\rfloor,t}]
,
\widetilde{\overline{\mathbf{u}}}_{t,\theta}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{it,\theta}
,
Y_{j^{\prime},t,1}=\sum_{s=1}^{t-3m-1}a_{s,t}\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},s}
and
Z_{j^{\prime},t,1}=\sum_{s=1\vee(t-3m)}^{t}a_{s,t}\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},s}
. Define
Y_{j^{\prime},t,2}
and
Z_{j^{\prime},t,2}
similarly by replacing
\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},s}
with
\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},s,\theta}
. Observe that
\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t}
and
\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t,\theta}
are independent of
Y_{j^{\prime},t,l}
for
l=1,2
.

We first consider
\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t}Z_{j^{\prime},t,1}-\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t,\theta}Z_{j^{\prime},t,2})
. Split
[T]
into blocks
B_{1},\ldots,B_{b_{T}}
with block size
4m
and define
W_{T,b}=\sum_{t\in B_{b}}(\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t}Z_{j^{\prime},t,1}-\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t,\theta}Z_{j^{\prime},t,2})
. Let
y_{T}
satisfy
y_{T}<z_{T}/2
and
T^{1+\delta/2}/y_{T}\to 0
. Since
W_{T,b}
and
W_{T,b^{\prime}}
are independent if
|b-b^{\prime}|>1
, by Lemma
A.2
(1), Lemma
A.6
(3) and Lemma
A.6
(2), for any
M>1
, there exists a constant
C_{q,M,\delta,\theta}
such that

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t}Z_{j^{\prime},t,1}-\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t,\theta}Z_{j^{\prime},t,2})-E\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t}Z_{j^{\prime},t,1}-\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t,\theta}Z_{j^{\prime},t,2})\right)\right|\geq y_{T}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{q,M,\delta,\theta}y_{T}^{-M}+\sum_{b=1}^{b_{T}}\Pr\left(|W_{T,b}-E(W_{T,b})|\geq y_{T}/C_{M,\delta}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{q,M,\delta,\theta}y_{T}^{-M}+C_{q,M,\delta,\theta}y_{T}^{-q/2}Tm^{q/2-1-(\alpha-1)\theta q/2}.

We next deal with the term
\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t}Y_{j^{\prime},t,1}-\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t,\theta}Y_{j^{\prime},t,2})
. Split
[T]
into blocks
B_{1}^{*},\ldots,B_{b_{T}}^{*}
with size
m
. Define
R_{T,b}=\sum_{t\in B_{b}^{*}}(\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t}Y_{j^{\prime},t,1}-\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t,\theta}Y_{j^{\prime},t,2})
. Let
\xi_{b}
be the
\sigma
-fields generated by
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l_{b}},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{l_{b-1}},\ldots)
, where
l_{b}=\max\{B_{b}^{*}\}
. Note that
\{R_{T,b}\}_{b\text{ is odd}}
is a martingale sequence with respect to
\{\xi_{b}\}_{b\text{ is odd}}
, and so are
\{R_{T,b}\}_{b\text{ is even}}
and
\{\xi_{b}\}_{b\text{ is even}}
. By Lemma 1 of
Haeusler (1984)
, for any
M>1
there exists a constant
C_{M,\delta}
such that

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t}Y_{j^{\prime},t,1}-\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t,\theta}Y_{j^{\prime},t,2})\right|\geq y_{T}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{M,\delta}y_{T}^{-M}+4\Pr\left(\left|\sum_{b=1}^{B_{T}^{*}}E(R_{T,b}^{2}\mid\xi_{b-2})\right|>y_{T}^{2}/(\log T)^{3/2}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{b=1}^{B_{T}^{*}}\Pr\left(\left|R_{T,b}\right|\geq y_{T}/\log T\right)

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle I_{10}+I_{11}+I_{12}.

Consider
I_{12}
first. Since
({\overline{u}}_{j,t},{\overline{u}}_{j,t,\theta})
and
(Y_{j^{\prime},t,1},Y_{j^{\prime},t,2})
are independent, by using similar arguments as Lemma
A.6
(2), we have

\|R_{T,b}\|_{q}\leq C_{q}(mT)^{q/2}m^{-(\alpha-1)\theta q},

which implies that

I_{12}\leq C_{q}y_{T}^{-q}(\log y_{T})^{q}T^{q/2+1}m^{q/2-1-(\alpha-1)\theta q}.

Consider
I_{11}
. Let
r_{j,s-t}=E(\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t}\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,s})
and
r_{j,s-t,\theta}=E(\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t,\theta}\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,s,\theta})
, then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{b=1}^{b_{T}^{*}}E(R_{T,b}^{2}\mid\xi_{b,2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sum_{b=1}^{b_{T}^{*}}\left[\sum_{s,t\in B_{b}^{*}}(r_{j,s-t}Y_{j^{\prime},s,1}Y_{j^{\prime},t,1}+r_{j,s-t,\theta}Y_{j^{\prime},s,2}Y_{j^{\prime},t,2})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq s\leq t\leq T}a_{j,s,t}\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},t}\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},s}+\sum_{1\leq s\leq t\leq T}a_{j,s,t,\theta}\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},t,\theta}\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},s,\theta},

in which
|a_{j,s,t}|\leq CT
and
|a_{j,s,t,\theta}|\leq CT
since
\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}|r_{j,l}|<\infty
and
\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}|r_{j,l,\theta}|<\infty
. Define
U(T,m,x_{T})=\sup_{\{a_{j,s,t}\}}\Pr\left(\left|\sum_{1\leq s\leq t\leq T}a_{j,s,t}\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},t}\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},s}-E\left(\sum_{1\leq s\leq t\leq T}a_{s,t}\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},t}\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},s}\right)\right|\geq x_{T}\right)
, where the supremum is taken over all arrays
\{a_{s,t}\}
such that
|a_{s,t}|\leq 1
. Therefore, we have

I_{11}\leq C_{\theta}U(T,m,y_{T}^{2}/(T(\log T)^{2}))+C_{\theta}U(T,\lfloor m^{\theta}\rfloor,y_{T}^{2}/(T(\log T)^{2})).

Combining the above analysis, we have shown that
U(T,m,z_{T})
is bounded from above by (up to a constant)

\displaystyle U(T,\lfloor m^{\theta}\rfloor,z_{T}-2y_{T})+U(T,m,y_{T}^{2}/(T(\log T)^{2}))

\displaystyle+U(T,\lfloor m^{\theta}\rfloor,y_{T}^{2}/(T(\log T)^{2}))+y_{T}^{-M}+y_{T}^{-q/2}Tm^{q/2-1-(\alpha-1)\theta q/2}

\displaystyle+y_{T}^{-q}(\log y_{T})^{q}T^{q/2+1}m^{q/2-1-(\alpha-1)\theta q}.

(B.1)

Since
\sup_{a_{j,s,t}}\|L_{T,j,j^{\prime}}-E(L_{T,j,j^{\prime}})-\widetilde{L}_{T,j,j^{\prime}}\|_{q/2}=O(T)
by Lemma
A.6
(3), by applying (
B.1
) recursively to deal with the term
U(T,m,y_{T}^{2}/(T(\log T)^{2}))
for
p
times such that
(y_{t}/T)^{-2^{p}q}=O(y_{T}^{-(M+1)})
, we have (up to a constant)

\displaystyle U(T,m,z_{T})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle U(T,\lfloor m^{\theta}\rfloor,z_{T}-2y_{T})+y_{T}^{-M}+y_{T}^{-q/2}Tm^{q/2-1-(\alpha-1)\theta q/2}

(B.2)

\displaystyle+y_{T}^{-q}(\log y_{T})^{q}T^{q/2+1}m^{q/2-1-(\alpha-1)\theta q}.

Using similar arguments, we can show for
1\leq m\leq 3

U(T,m,z_{T}/(2l_{T}))\leq O\left(z_{T}^{-M}+z_{T}^{-q/2}T\log T+z_{T}^{-q}(\log z_{T})^{q+1}T^{q/2+1}\right).

Using (
B.2
) for
l_{T}-1
times, we have

\displaystyle U(T,m,z_{T})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)(\log z_{T})^{q+1}\left(z_{T}^{-q/2}T+z_{T}^{-q}T^{q/2+1}\right)(m^{q/2-1-(\alpha-1)\theta q/2}+1).

(B.3)

Plugging (
B.3
) back into (
B.1
) for the terms
U(T,m,y_{T}^{2}/(T(\log T)^{2}))
and

U(T,\lfloor m^{\theta}\rfloor,y_{T}^{2}/(T(\log T)^{2}))
and using
T^{1+\delta/2}/y_{T}\to 0
, we have

\displaystyle U(T,m,z_{T})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle U(T,\lfloor m^{\theta}\rfloor,z_{T}-2y_{T})+O(1)z_{T}^{-q}T(m^{q/2-1-(\alpha-1)\theta q/2}+1).

(B.4)

Finally, by using (
B.4
) for
l_{T}-1
times, we obtain

U(T,m,z_{T})\leq O(1)z_{T}^{-q}T\log T(m^{q/2-1-(\alpha-1)\theta q/2}+1).

The proof is now complete.

(2). For
l\geq 1
, define
m_{T,l}=\lfloor T^{\theta^{l}}\rfloor
,
\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{it,l}=E[\mathbf{u}_{it}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-m_{T,l},t}]
,
\widetilde{\overline{\mathbf{u}}}_{t,l}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{it,l}
and
Q_{NT,l}=\sum_{1\leq s<t\leq T}a_{t-s}\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t,l}\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},s,l}
. Let
l_{T}=\lceil-\log(\log T)/\log\theta\rceil
and thus
m_{T,l_{T}}\leq e
. By using Lemmas
A.6
(2) and (3), we have

\Pr(|L_{NT,j,j'} - E(L_{NT,j,j'}) - Q_{NT,1} + E(Q_{NT,1})|\geq x_T/l_T)\leq C_{q,\theta}(\log T)^{1/2}x_{T}^{-q/2}(T\ell)^{q/4}T^{-(\alpha-1)\theta q/2}.

Let
l_{T}^{\prime}
be the smallest
l
such that
m_{T,l}\leq\ell/4
. For
1\leq l\leq l_{T}^{\prime}
, split
[1,T]
into blocks
B_{1}^{l},\ldots,B_{b_{T,l}}^{l}
with size
\ell+m_{T,l}
. Define
W_{T,l,b}=\sum_{t\in B_{b}^{l}}\sum_{1\leq s\leq t}a_{t-s}\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t,l}\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},s,l}
and

W_{T,l,b}^{\prime}=\sum_{t\in B_{b}^{l}}\sum_{1\leq s\leq t}a_{t-s}\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t,l+1}\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},s,l+1}.

Then, by using Lemma
A.2
(1) and using Lemma
A.6
(3), we have for any
C>2

\displaystyle\Pr(|Q_{NT,l} - E(Q_{NT,l}) - (Q_{NT,l+1} - E(Q_{NT,l+1}))|\geq x_T/(2l_T))

(B.5)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{b=1}^{b_{T,l}}\Pr(|W_{T,l,b} - E(W_{T,l,b}) - (W_{T,l,b}' - E(W_{T,l,b}'))|\geq x_T/(Cl_T))

\displaystyle+O(1)\left[\frac{T\ell l_{T}^{2}}{x_{T}^{2}}\right]^{C/4}.

Note that for any
M>1
, there exists a constant
C_{M,\delta,\theta}
such that the second term in the right hand of (
B.5
) is less than
C_{M,\delta,\theta}x_{T}^{-M}
. For the first term, by using Lemmas
A.6
(2) and (3), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{b=1}^{b_{T,l}}\Pr(|W_{T,l,b} - E(W_{T,l,b}) - (W_{T,l,b}' - E(W_{T,l,b'}))|\geq x_T/(Cl_T))

(B.6)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)Tm_{T,l}^{-1}\sqrt{\log T}x_{T}^{-q/2}(m_{T,l}\ell)^{q/4}m_{T,l+1}^{-(\alpha-1)q/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)x_{T}^{-q/2}\sqrt{\log T}T\ell^{q/4}m_{T,l}^{q/4-1-(\alpha-1)\theta q/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)x_{T}^{-q/2}\sqrt{\log T}\left((T\ell)^{q/4}T^{-(\alpha-1)\theta q/2}+T\ell^{q/2-1-(\alpha-1)\theta q/2}\right).

Combining (
B.5
) and (
B.6
), we have

\displaystyle\Pr(|L_{NT,j,j'} - E(L_{NT,j,j'})|\geq x_T)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Pr(|Q_{NT,l_T'} - E(Q_{NT,l_T'})|\geq x_T/2)+O(1)x_{T}^{-M}

\displaystyle+O(1)x_{T}^{-q/2}\sqrt{\log T}\left((T\ell)^{q/4}T^{-(\alpha-1)\theta q/2}+T\ell^{q/2-1-(\alpha-1)\theta q/2}\right).

For
\Pr(|Q_{NT,l_T'} - E(Q_{NT,l_T'})|\geq x_T/2)
, split
[T]
into blocks
B_{1},\ldots,B_{b_{T}}
with block size
2\ell
and define

W_{T,l_{T}^{\prime},b}=\sum_{t\in B_{b}}\sum_{1\leq s\leq t}a_{t-s}\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j,t,l_{T}^{\prime}}\widetilde{\overline{u}}_{j^{\prime},s,l_{T}^{\prime}}
. Similarly, we have

\Pr(|Q_{NT,l_T'} - E(Q_{NT,l_T'})|\geq x_T/2)\leq\sum_{b=1}^{b_{T}}\Pr(|W_{T,l_T',b} - E(W_{T,l_T',b})|\geq x_T/C)+Cx_{T}^{-M}.

By using part (1) of this lemma, we have

\Pr(|W_{T,l_T',b} - E(W_{T,l_T',b})|\geq x_T/C)\leq O(1)x_{T}^{-q/2}(\log T)(\ell^{q/2-(\alpha-1)\theta q/2}+\ell),

which follows that

\Pr(|Q_{NT,l_T'} - E(Q_{NT,l_T'})|\geq x_T/2)\leq O(1)x_{T}^{-q/2}(T\log T)(\ell^{q/2-1-(\alpha-1)\theta q/2}+1).

The proof is now complete.
∎

Proof of Lemma
A.8
.

(1). By using Lemma
1
(2) and letting
x_{T}=M\sqrt{T\ell\log d}
for some sufficient large but fixed
M
and
\theta=1/(\alpha-1)
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{1\leq k,l\leq d}\left|\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}a((t-s)/\ell)\left(\overline{u}_{k,t}\overline{u}_{l,s}-E(\overline{u}_{k,t}\overline{u}_{l,s})\right)\right|\geq x_{T}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k,l=1}^{d}\Pr\left(\left|\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}a((t-s)/\ell)\left(\overline{u}_{k,t}\overline{u}_{l,s}-E(\overline{u}_{k,t}\overline{u}_{l,s})\right)\right|\geq x_{T}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)d^{2}x_{T}^{-q/2}\log T(T^{-q/4}\ell^{q/4}+T\ell^{-1}+T)+O(1)d^{2-M}+O(1)d^{2}T^{-M}\to 0

if
d^{2}T\log T/(T\ell\log d)^{q/4}\to 0
. Hence, we have proved

\max_{1\leq k,l\leq d}\left|T^{-1}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}a((t-s)/\ell)\left(\overline{u}_{k,t}\overline{u}_{l,s}-E(\overline{u}_{k,t}\overline{u}_{l,s})\right)\right|=O_{P}(\sqrt{\ell\log d/T}).

(2). By using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k,l,l^{\prime}\leq d}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}x_{k,it}x_{k,jt}x_{l,it}x_{l^{\prime},jt}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k,l\leq d}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{k,it}x_{l,it}\right)^{2}\right|\leq\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{k,it}^{2}\right\}^{2}\right|=O_{P}(1).

Hence, by using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, if
s^{2}\sqrt{\ell\log d/T}<\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k,l\leq d}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}a((t-s)/\ell)\left(\widehat{\overline{u}}_{k,t}-\overline{u}_{k,t}\right)\left(\widehat{\overline{u}}_{l,s}-\overline{u}_{l,s}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(2\ell+1)\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\widehat{\overline{u}}_{k,t}-\overline{u}_{k,t}\right)^{2}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N(2\ell+1)\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\left|\sum_{1\leq l,l^{\prime}\leq d}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}x_{k,it}x_{k,jt}x_{l,it}x_{l^{\prime},jt}(\widehat{\beta}_{l}-\beta_{l})(\widehat{\beta}_{l^{\prime}}-\beta_{l^{\prime}})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N(2\ell+1)|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{1}^{2}\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\max_{l,l^{\prime}\in J}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}x_{k,it}x_{k,jt}x_{l,it}x_{l^{\prime},jt}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}(\ell s^{2}\log d/T)=O_{P}(\sqrt{\ell\log d/T}).

The proof of part (2) is now complete.

(3). By using similar arguments of Lemma
1
(2), if
\frac{d^{3}T\log T}{(T\ell\log d)^{q/4}}\to 0
we have

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k,l,l^{\prime}\leq d}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}a((t-s)/\ell)\left(N^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{k,it}x_{l^{\prime},it}\overline{u}_{l,s}-E((N^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{k,it}x_{l^{\prime},it}\overline{u}_{l,s})\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}(\sqrt{\ell\log d/T}).

Since
E(N^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{k,it}x_{l,it}\overline{u}_{l,s})=0
(implied by
E(e_{it}\mid\mathbf{X})=0
), then we have

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k,l\leq d}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}a((t-s)/\ell)\left(\widehat{\overline{u}}_{k,t}-\overline{u}_{k,t}\right)\overline{u}_{l,s}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k,l,l^{\prime}\leq d}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}a((t-s)/\ell)N^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{k,it}x_{l^{\prime},it}\overline{u}_{l,s}\right|\times|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}|_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}(\sqrt{\ell\log d/T})\times O_{P}(s\sqrt{\log d/(NT)})=o_{P}(\sqrt{\ell\log d/T}).

The proof is now complete.
∎

B.2
Results of Section
3

Lemma B.1
.

Let (
2.4
) and Assumptions
1
,
2
and
5
hold, and
s=o(\max(T^{-1/2},N^{-1/2})NT/\log d)
. Then we have

1.

|\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{X}_{J,t}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}^{(l)}}\mathbf{e}_{t}|_{\infty}=O_{P}\left(\max(\sqrt{s}\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\right)
;

2.

|\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{X}_{J,t}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}^{(l)}}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}\mathbf{f}_{0t}|_{\infty}=O_{P}\left(\max(\sqrt{s}\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\right)
;

3.

|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(l)}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}|_{\max}=O_{P}(\max(\sqrt{s}\omega_{1},\omega_{2})
,

where
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(l)}=\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{X}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}^{(l)}}\mathbf{X}_{t}
and
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}=\plim\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{X}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{t}
.

Proposition B.1
.

Let (
2.4
) and Assumptions
1
,
2
and
5
hold with
s=o(\max(T^{-1/2},N^{-1/2})NT/\log d)
. Then the following results hold.

1.

|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{2}=O_{P}\left(\max(\sqrt{s}\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\right)
and
\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0}|_{F}=O_{P}\left(\max(\sqrt{s}\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\right)
.

2.

|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}-\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}|_{F}/\sqrt{N}=O_{P}\left(\max(\sqrt{s}\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\right)
for some rotation matrix
\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}
depending on
(\mathbf{F}_{0},\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0})
.

Proof of Lemma
B.1
.

(1). Write

\left|\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{X}_{J,t}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}^{(l)}}\mathbf{e}_{t}\right|_{\infty}\leq\left|\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{X}_{J,t}^{\top}\mathbf{e}_{t}\right|_{\infty}+\left|\frac{1}{N^{2}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{X}_{J,t}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}^{(l)}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}^{(l),\top}\mathbf{e}_{t}\right|_{\infty}.

By using Lemma
A.4
(1), the first term is of order
O_{P}(\sqrt{\log d/(NT)})
. For the second term, by using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{N^{2}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{X}_{J,t}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}^{(l)}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}^{(l),\top}\mathbf{e}_{t}\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\{\max_{j\in J}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left|\mathbf{X}_{j,t}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}^{(l)}/N\right|_{F}^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\left\{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{i}^{(l)}e_{it}\right|_{F}^{2}\right\}^{1/2}.

Note that
\left|\mathbf{X}_{j,t}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}^{(l)}/N\right|_{F}^{2}\leq\widehat{r}N^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{j,it}^{2}
and

\max_{j\in J}\left|\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(x_{j,it}^{2}-E(x_{j,it}^{2})\right)\right|=O_{P}(\sqrt{\log d/(NT)}),

we then have

\displaystyle\max_{j\in J}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left|\mathbf{X}_{j,t}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}^{(l)}/N\right|_{F}^{2}\leq\max_{j\in J}\frac{r}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{j,it}^{2}=O_{P}(1).

In addition, by using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{i}^{(l)}e_{it}\right|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{T}|\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}|_{F}^{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0i}e_{it}\right|_{F}^{2}+\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0i}-\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{i}^{(l)})e_{it}\right|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O_{P}(1/N)+\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left|\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0i}-\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{i}^{(l)}\right|_{F}^{2}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}e_{it}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}(1/N+\max(s\omega_{1}^{2},\omega_{2}^{2})).

Combining the above analyses, we have proved part (1).

(2). Note that
|\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}^{(l)}}|_{2}=1
, then

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{X}_{J,t}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}^{(l)}}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}\mathbf{f}_{0t}\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{j\in J}\left|\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{X}_{j,t}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}^{(l)}}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}^{(l)})\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}^{-1}\mathbf{f}_{t}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{j\in J}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}|N^{-1/2}\mathbf{X}_{j,t}|_{F}|\mathbf{f}_{t}|_{F}|\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}^{(l)}}|_{2}N^{-1/2}|\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}^{(l)}|_{F}|\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}^{-1}|_{F}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}(\max(\sqrt{s}\omega_{1},\omega_{2})).

(3). Note that
\left|\mathbf{P}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}-\mathbf{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}}\right|_{F}=O_{P}(\max(\sqrt{s}\omega_{1},\omega_{2}))
by the proof of Proposition
B.1
, and by using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{X}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}^{(l)}}\mathbf{X}_{t}-\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{X}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{t}\right|_{\max}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{P}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}-\mathbf{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}}\right|_{F}\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{j,it}^{2}=O_{P}(\max(\sqrt{s}\omega_{1},\omega_{2})).

In addition by using Lemma
A.4
, we can show

\left|\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{X}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{t}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\right|_{\max}=O_{P}(\sqrt{\log d/(NT)}).

The proof is now complete.
∎

Proof of Proposition
B.1
.

(1). We first evaluate the difference of the objective function defined in (
3.3
) at
(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}})
and
(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0},\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})
. Thus, by the definition of
(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}})
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2NT}\left(|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\mathrm{vec}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}})|_{2}^{2}-|\mathbf{e}|_{2}^{2}\right)+\omega_{1}(|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|_{1}-|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{1})+\frac{\omega_{2}}{\sqrt{NT}}\left(|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}|_{*}-|\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0}|_{*}\right)

\displaystyle\eqqcolon

\displaystyle J_{7}+J_{8}+J_{9}\leq 0,

where the definitions of
J_{7}
,
J_{8}
and
J_{9}
are self-evident.

Consider
J_{7}
. Note that the event
\mathcal{A}_{NT}=\left\{\left|\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{e}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\right|_{\infty}\leq\omega_{1}/2\right\}
holds with probability larger than
C_{NT}
.
Conditional on
\mathcal{A}_{NT}
, using Assumption
5
.1,
(\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}))^{\top}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{B})=\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{B})
and
\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{B})\leq|\mathbf{A}|_{2}|\mathbf{B}|_{*}
, we have

\displaystyle J_{7}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2NT}|\mathbf{X}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0})+\mathrm{vec}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})|_{2}^{2}-|\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{e}|_{\infty}|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{1}-\frac{1}{NT}|\mathbf{E}|_{2}|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0}|_{*}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2NT}|\mathbf{X}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0})+\mathrm{vec}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})|_{2}^{2}-\frac{\omega_{1}}{2}|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{1}-\frac{\omega_{2}}{2\sqrt{NT}}|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0}|_{*}.

For
J_{8}
, we have
J_{8}=\omega_{1}(|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{J}|_{1}+|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{J^{c}}|_{1}-|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,J}|_{1})\geq\omega_{1}(|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{J^{c}}|_{1}-|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{J}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,J}|_{1})
.

Consider
J_{9}
. Let
\mathbb{P}(\boldsymbol{\Xi})=\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{U}_{0,[r]}}\boldsymbol{\Xi}\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{V}_{0,[r]}}
and
\mathbb{M}(\boldsymbol{\Xi})=\boldsymbol{\Xi}-\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{U}_{0,[r]}}\boldsymbol{\Xi}\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{V}_{0,[r]}}
. Note that
|\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{B}|_{*}=|\mathbf{A}|_{*}+|\mathbf{B}|_{*}
if
\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{B}=0
and
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}^{\top}=0
, and thus we have

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}|_{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0}+\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0}|_{*}=|\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0}+\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})+\mathbb{M}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})|_{*}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0}+\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})|_{*}-|\mathbb{M}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})|_{*}=|\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0}|_{*}+|\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})|_{*}-|\mathbb{M}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})|_{*},

which follows that

J_{9}\geq\frac{\omega_{2}}{\sqrt{NT}}|\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})|_{*}-\frac{\omega_{2}}{\sqrt{NT}}|\mathbb{M}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})|_{*}.

Combing the above analyses, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT}|\mathbf{X}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0})+\mathrm{vec}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})|_{2}^{2}+\omega_{1}|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{J^{c}}|_{1}+\frac{\omega_{2}}{\sqrt{NT}}|\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})|_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3\omega_{1}|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{J}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,J}|_{1}+3\frac{\omega_{2}}{\sqrt{NT}}|\mathbb{M}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})|_{*}.

Hence,
(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})\in\mathbb{C}
and thus by Assumption
5
.2, we have

\displaystyle\kappa_{c}|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{2}^{2}+\kappa_{c}\frac{1}{NT}|\mathrm{vec}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{NT}|\mathbf{X}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0})+\mathrm{vec}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})|_{2}^{2}.

In addition, since
|\boldsymbol{\Xi}|_{F}^{2}=|\mathbb{P}(\boldsymbol{\Xi})|_{F}^{2}+|\mathbb{M}(\boldsymbol{\Xi})|_{F}^{2}+2\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{P}(\boldsymbol{\Xi})^{\top}\mathbb{M}(\boldsymbol{\Xi}))=|\mathbb{P}(\boldsymbol{\Xi})|_{F}^{2}+|\mathbb{M}(\boldsymbol{\Xi})|_{F}^{2}
,
|\boldsymbol{\Xi}|_{*}^{2}\leq|\boldsymbol{\Xi}|_{F}^{2}\mathrm{rank}(\boldsymbol{\Xi})
and
\mathrm{rank}(\mathbb{M}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0}))\leq 2r
(which we will prove in the following), we have

\displaystyle\kappa_{c}|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{2}^{2}+\kappa_{c}\frac{1}{NT}|\mathrm{vec}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3\omega_{1}|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{J}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0,J}|_{1}+3\frac{\omega_{2}}{\sqrt{NT}}|\mathbb{M}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})|_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3\sqrt{s}\omega_{1}|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{2}+3\sqrt{2r}\frac{\omega_{2}}{\sqrt{NT}}|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0}|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max\left\{6\sqrt{s}\omega_{1},6\sqrt{2r}\omega_{2}\right\}\sqrt{|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{NT}|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0}|_{F}^{2}}.

We now prove
\mathrm{rank}(\mathbb{M}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0}))\leq 2r
. Define the matrix
\boldsymbol{\Delta}=\mathbf{U}_{0}^{\top}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})\mathbf{V}_{0}=\mathbf{U}_{0}^{\top}\mathbb{M}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})\mathbf{V}_{0}+\mathbf{U}_{0}^{\top}\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})\mathbf{V}_{0}
, and write it in block form as

\boldsymbol{\Delta}=\left[\begin{matrix}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{11}&\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{12}\\
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{21}&\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{22}\end{matrix}\right],

where
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{11}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
and
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{11}\in\mathbb{R}^{(T-r)\times(N-r)}
. Since
\mathbf{U}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{U}_{0,[r]}}=[\mathbf{0}_{T\times r},\mathbf{U}_{0,[T-r]}]^{\top}
and
\mathbf{V}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{V}_{0,[r]}}=[\mathbf{0}_{N\times r},\mathbf{V}_{0,[N-r]}]^{\top}
, we have

\mathbf{U}_{0}^{\top}\mathbb{M}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})\mathbf{V}_{0}=\left[\begin{matrix}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{11}&\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{12}\\
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{21}&\mathbf{0}\end{matrix}\right],

which implies that

\displaystyle\mathrm{rank}(\mathbb{M}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0}))=\mathrm{rank}(\mathbf{U}_{0}^{\top}\mathbb{M}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0})\mathbf{V}_{0})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{rank}\left(\left[\begin{matrix}\mathbf{0}&\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{12}\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{0}\end{matrix}\right]\right)+\mathrm{rank}\left(\left[\begin{matrix}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{11}&\mathbf{0}\\
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{21}&\mathbf{0}\end{matrix}\right]\right)\leq 2r.

Hence, we have proved

|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}|_{2}\leq\max\left\{\frac{6\sqrt{s}\omega_{1}}{\kappa_{c}},\frac{6\sqrt{2r}\omega_{2}}{\kappa_{c}}\right\}

and

\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0}|_{F}\leq\max\left\{\frac{6\sqrt{s}\omega_{1}}{\kappa_{c}},\frac{6\sqrt{2r}\omega_{2}}{\kappa_{c}}\right\}

with probability larger than
1-C_{1}\left(\frac{dT^{1-q/2}}{(\log d)^{q/2}}+d^{-C_{2}}\right)
.

(2). Let
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\lambda}=\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}/N
,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{f}=\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}/T
and
\widehat{n}_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\widehat{n}_{r}
be the
r
nonzero eigenvalues of
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0}/(NT)=\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{f}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}^{\top}/N
. Note that the nonzero eigenvalues of
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{f}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}^{\top}/N
are the same as those of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\lambda}^{1/2}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{f}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\lambda}^{1/2}
. Then with probability approaching to 1 (w.p.a.1), for some
0<C<\infty
and
1\leq j\leq r
, by using the Weyl’s theorem, we have

|\widehat{n}_{j}-n_{j}|\leq|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\lambda}^{1/2}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{f}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\lambda}^{1/2}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\lambda}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{f}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\lambda}^{1/2}|_{2}\leq C(T^{-1/2}+N^{-1/2}),

which also implies that
|\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0}|_{2}/\sqrt{NT}=\sqrt{n_{1}+O_{P}(T^{-1/2}+N^{-1/2})}
.

Let
\widetilde{n}_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\widetilde{n}_{N\wedge T}
be the eigenvalues of
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}^{\top}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}/(NT)
. Again, by using the Weyl’s theorem, part (1) of this lemma and the condition
s=o\left(\max(T^{-1/2},N^{-1/2})NT/\log d\right)
, we have

|\widetilde{n}_{j}-n_{j}|\leq C\cdot\max(\sqrt{s}\omega_{1},\omega_{2})=o(C\sqrt{\omega_{2}})

w.p.a.1 for all
j\geq 1
and
|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}|_{2}/\sqrt{NT}=\sqrt{n_{1}+o_{P}(C\sqrt{\omega_{2}})}
. In addition, since
\psi_{j}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}})/\sqrt{NT}=\sqrt{\widetilde{n}_{j}}
, we have
\psi_{j}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}})/\sqrt{NT}\geq\sqrt{n_{j}-C\sqrt{\omega_{2}}}
w.p.a.1 for
1\leq j\leq r
. The above results imply that
\psi_{r}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}})\geq(\omega_{2}\sqrt{NT}|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}|_{2})^{1/2}
and
\psi_{r+1}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}})<(\omega_{2}\sqrt{NT}|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}|_{2})^{1/2}
w.p.a.1.

We next prove
|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}-\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}|_{F}/\sqrt{N}=O_{P}\left(\max(\sqrt{s}\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\right)
for some rotation matrix
\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}
depending on
(\mathbf{F}_{0},\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0})
. Let
\mathbf{V}
be the
r\times r
matrix whose columns are the eigenvectors of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\lambda}^{1/2}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{f}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\lambda}^{1/2}
. Then
\mathbf{D}=\mathbf{V}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\lambda}^{1/2}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{f}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\lambda}^{1/2}\mathbf{V}
is a diagonal matrix of the eigenvalues of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\lambda}^{1/2}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{f}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\lambda}^{1/2}
. Let
\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}=\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\lambda}^{-1/2}\mathbf{V}
and then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT}\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{f}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\lambda}^{1/2}\mathbf{V}=\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\lambda}^{-1/2}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\lambda}^{1/2}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{f}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\lambda}^{1/2}\mathbf{V}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\lambda}^{-1/2}\mathbf{V}\mathbf{D}=\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}\mathbf{D}.

In addition, we have
(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{H}})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}/N=\mathbf{I}_{r}
and thus the columns of
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}/\sqrt{N}
are the eigenvectors of
\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Xi}
with the associated eigenvalues in
\mathbf{D}
. In addition, conditional on the event
\widehat{r}=r
, by using Davis-Kahan sin(
\Theta
) theorem, we have

|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}-\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}|_{F}/\sqrt{T}\leq\frac{(NT)^{-1}|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}^{\top}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}-{\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{0}}|_{2}}{\min_{j\leq r}\min(|\widehat{n}_{j-1}-\widetilde{n}_{j}|,|\widetilde{n}_{j}-\widehat{n}_{j+1}|)}=O_{P}(\max(\sqrt{s}\omega_{1},\omega_{2})).

In addition, we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{P}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}-\mathbf{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}}\right|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}^{\top}/N-\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}^{\top}\right|_{F}+\left|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}-\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}\right|_{F}^{2}/N

\displaystyle+2\left|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}-\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}\right|_{F}\left|\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}\right|_{F}/N=O_{P}(\max(\sqrt{s}\omega_{1},\omega_{2})).

The proof is now complete.
∎
